I base my proposal on simple truths engraved on the basis of the State of Latvia, namely: “Persons belonging to minorities have the right to preserve and develop their language, ethnic and cultural specificities.” This is Article 114 of the Satversme. ‘ Everyone has the right to join associations, political parties and other public organisations.’ This is Article 102 of the Satversme. I draw attention to the fact that in the budget of the Ministry of Culture one of the main activities of the programme “Industry Management and Policy Planning” is (I quote): “Implementation of cooperation with non-governmental organisations of minorities”. This main activity of the programme is specified in the sub-programme ‘Societal cohesion measures’, and the activity of this sub-programme is (concluded): ‘Strengthening national identity, including strengthening the cultural specificity of minorities’. Meanwhile, at the meeting of the Committee on Budgets and Finance (taxes), Members learned that, despite the creation of a hierarchy of all these good intentions, in the dry balance between the priority measures that were applied for by the Ministry of Culture, however, they did not receive support from the Cabinet, there was also a priority measure to preserve the cultural speciality of minorities, and I therefore least blame the Minister of Culture Puntulis for not providing sufficient funds for the sub-programme. However, in view of the significant increase in funding from the Ministry of Culture and the Society Integration Fund last year for a period of three years, I propose the creation of a sub-programme "Support for Latvian Minorities NGOs", to which I will allocate a named amount of EUR 120,000. This amount can be divided between the four existing NGO support centres, which have a great deal of experience in useful financial education and a high level of public integration. I call for my proposal to be supported.

ParlaMint-LV_2020-12-01-PT13-2153-U142	In 2021, 1.5 million euros are available for the Latvian NGO fund competition for non-governmental organisations. The competition is attended by associations or foundations registered in the Republic of Latvia that comply with the Law on Associations and Foundations, whose scope: development of civil society, solving major societal problems in the interest of the wider public, achieving national goals by promoting public participation. Minorities associations may also submit their applications under the general competition procedure. The 2021 budget includes cooperative activities involving minority societies that are already included in the budget. Support for the organisation of creative camps and professional competence development events of minority collective managers - 13 840 EUR. The support programme for the preservation and development of minority cultures, including the events of the Association of Latvian National Cultural Associations – 40 thousand euros. Latvian minority forum and culture festival - 10 thousand euros. Total budget: EUR 63 840. The Ministry of Culture has so far actively supported non-governmental organisations of minority culture and has also requested greater funding for 2021, but unfortunately, the first priority, where we were forced to make concessions, is to exit public media from the advertising market. A very important measure, without which the country cannot do so, too, unfortunately, it is calling for these 8.3 million. The other priorities were therefore subordinated.

ParlaMint-LV_2020-12-01-PT13-2153-U490	Let's start a debate. Name to Member Igor Pimenov.
ParlaMint-LV_2020-12-01-PT13-2153-U491	I ask you to combine the times of the debate.
ParlaMint-LV_2020-12-01-PT13-2153-U492	Your time is combined. Please!
ParlaMint-LV_2020-12-01-PT13-2153-U493	Thank you. So, as I said, not the financial markets, but the country has to put pressure on economic development. And economic growth needs an economic engine. I propose to set up the State Development Bank of Latvia with a fixed capital of EUR 100 million to ensure state support for targeted programmes. According to banking supervisory standards, a bank may grant loans or provide guarantees in accordance with capital adequacy requirements. This means that for every million euro in capital, a development bank can provide guarantees or loans of between eight and 10 million euro. Therefore, EUR 100 million of capital will enable the bank to raise up to EUR 1 billion for various state aid programmes. Development Bank money will be allocated to the creation of new assets. Thus, it will not generate inflation and will not contribute to an increase in imports. I am opposed to the fact that the Development Finance Institution Altum has already increased its reserve capital by EUR 100 million to finance the crisis guarantee programme and the crisis loan programme. And why else do we set up a development bank? Yes, Altum can borrow and lend money, but without a multiplier. Altum cannot grant billions of euros in loans against an increase in capital of 100 million because it is not a bank. Altum's not a bank license. Altum can issue guarantees to commercial banks, which in turn can issue loans in proportion of 1 to 10. However, for what purpose will be given - only the commercial bank will know. But it is in the interests of the country to grant credits directly for development. Moreover, a state-owned development bank operating in line with international banking supervisory standards has the ability to borrow financial resources on the capital market not only with but also without state guarantees. Latvia needs a national development bank, a bank such as that already exists in some Member States of the European Union, which are indeed the main sources of energy for its economy. I ask you to support my proposal.

The differentiated non-expensive minimum as a means of simulating this struggle stands out as particularly challenging. It is little more difficult to administer, explain and understand even more to taxpayers the differentiated non-taxable minimum is a very weak means of reducing social inequalities, because it will affect all workers whose monthly wages do not exceed €1,800, but it will not apply to those with a higher income, which is the main reason why this minimum is powerless to reduce inequality. All these huge platforms are those where our advertisers migrate as well, and the money of the Republic of Latvia goes there. It is this digital tax, and we still do not have it at the moment.
